Q1. Tens digit \(7\) and digit sum \(15\) are fixed. What is the number?
A. \(77\)
B. \(80\)
C. \(78\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
7+u=15\Rightarrow u=8
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 78.

\[
N=10\times 7+8=78
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 78 is 15.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. The tens place has \(5\), and all digits add to \(7\). Which number is it?
A. \(51\)
B. \(54\)
C. \(52\)
D. \(53\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
5+u=7\Rightarrow u=2
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 52.

\[
N=10\times 5+2=52
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 52 is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(52\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=52
\]

Fast result: \(52\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule
---
Q3. The number \(554x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(105\) and \(198\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 105 is 108.

\[
9\times 12=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 198 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 198.

\[
198=9\times 22+0
\]

\[
198-0=198
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{198-108}{9}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{198}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{104}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-11=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(106\) and \(213\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 106 is 108.

\[
9\times 12=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 207 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 213.

\[
213=9\times 23+6
\]

\[
213-6=207
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{207-108}{9}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{213}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{105}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-11=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q6. Between \(204\) and \(334\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 204 is 204.

\[
6\times 34=204
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 334.

\[
334=6\times 55+4
\]

\[
334-4=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{330-204}{6}+1=21+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 22.
Hence, there are 22 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{334}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{203}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
55-33=22
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q7. Bells ring at intervals of \(36\) and \(153\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(673\)
B. \(581\)
C. \(612\)
D. \(643\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

\[
153=3^{2}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,153)=612
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 612.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(612\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,153)=\frac{36\times 153}{\operatorname{HCF}(36,153)}=612
\]

Fast result: \(612\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge
---
Q8. For the number \(1440\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)
C. \(36\)
D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(1440)=(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)=36
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. For the number \(1440\), find the count of odd divisors?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

\[
O=6
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count
---
Q15. For the number \(480\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(1814\)
B. \(1361\)
C. \(1512\)
D. \(1663\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---
Q17. Two cyclic events repeat every \(88\) and \(48\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(554\)
B. \(581\)
C. \(502\)
D. \(528\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
88=2^{3}\times 11
\]

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=528
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 528.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(528\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(88,48)=\frac{88\times 48}{\operatorname{HCF}(88,48)}=528
\]

Fast result: \(528\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q25. When \(3^{58}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(58-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{58}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(58-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
3^{58}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(65-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
13^{65}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(65-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[
6^{65}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q41. Tens digit \(5\) and ones digit \(2\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(26\)
B. \(29\)
C. \(27\)
D. \(28\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+2=52
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+5=25
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-2|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-2|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[

\[

\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q52. Between \(168\) and \(307\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 168 is 168.

\[
6\times 28=168
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 307.

\[
307=6\times 51+1
\]

\[
307-1=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{306-168}{6}+1=23+1=24
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 24.
Hence, there are 24 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{307}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{167}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
51-27=24
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=78
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 78.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(78\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,78)=\frac{78\times 78}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,78)}=78
\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

p=(70-1)\bmod 8+1=6
\]

\[
2^{70}\equiv 13\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

Q88. The number \(96x49\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=8
\]

\[
8+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
8+x=11
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
8+3=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q92. When \(7^{28}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(28-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{28}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q97. Between \(115\) and \(223\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 115 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 210 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 223.

\[
223=15\times 14+13
\]

\[
223-13=210
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{210-120}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{223}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{114}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
14-7=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
p=(91-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
3^{91}\equiv 7\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q113. Number \(86\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+6=86
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+8=68
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-6|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-6|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q123. Between \(226\) and \(349\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 226 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 349.

\[
349=15\times 23+4
\]

\[
349-4=345
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-240}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{349}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{225}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-15=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q136. The number \(77x92\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 25; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
25+x=27
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
25+2=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

7\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(38-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(38-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{38}\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q152. Between \(171\) and \(340\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 171 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 340.

\[
340=18\times 18+16
\]

\[
340-16=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-180}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{340}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{170}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-9=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q153. Two cyclic events repeat every \(40\) and \(104\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?

Answer: D

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=520
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 520.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(520\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,104)=\frac{40\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,104)}=520
\]

Fast result: \(520\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Answer: A

Explanation:

\[

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[

\]

\[
(57-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q180. Between \(193\) and \(337\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 193 is 200.

\[
8\times 25=200
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 337.

\[
337=8\times 42+1
\]

\[
337-1=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{336-200}{8}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{337}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{192}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-24=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q181. Two cyclic events repeat every \(72\) and \(180\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(378\)
B. \(396\)
C. \(342\)
D. \(360\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

\[
180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=360
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 360.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(360\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(72,180)=\frac{72\times 180}{\operatorname{HCF}(72,180)}=360
\]

Fast result: \(360\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 144 is 150.

\[
15\times 10=150
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 255 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 256.

\[
256=15\times 17+1
\]

\[
256-1=255
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{255-150}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{256}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{143}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-9=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q199. When \(3^{72}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(72-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{72}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(72-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{72}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(66-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{66}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(66-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
3^{66}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_modular_cycle

Q229. Between \(130\) and \(233\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 130 is 135.

\[
15\times 9=135
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 233.

\[
233=15\times 15+8
\]

\[
233-8=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{225-135}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{233}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{129}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-8=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q235. For the number \(20\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(9600\)
B. \(7200\)
C. \(8000\)
D. \(8800\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
20=2^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(20)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 20 d=20^{d(20)/2}=20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 8000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
20^{6/2}=8000
\]

Fast result: \(8000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q253. Reduce the power \(7^{107}\) by its remainder pattern. What is the remainder on division by \(19\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(107-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{107}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(107-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{107}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q259. The number \(36x49\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=2
\]

\[
2+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
2+x=11
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=9\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
2+9=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Answer: B

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(234,108)=1404
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1404.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1404\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(234,108)=\frac{234\times 108}{\operatorname{HCF}(234,108)}=1404
\]

Fast result: \(1404\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

A. \(27\)
B. \(30\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 121 is 126.

\[
6\times 21=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 290.

\[
290=6\times 48+2
\]

\[
290-2=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{288-126}{6}+1=27+1=28
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 28.
Hence, there are 28 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(28\).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q281. Power \(2^{71}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(71-1)\bmod 10+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
2^{71}\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(71-1)\bmod 10+1=1
\]

\[
2^{71}\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q287. Between \(179\) and \(305\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 179 is 184.

\[
8\times 23=184
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 304 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 305.

\[
305=8\times 38+1
\]

\[
305-1=304
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{304-184}{8}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{305}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{178}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
38-22=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(2693\)
Answer: B

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[
204=2^{2}\times 3\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=2244
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 2244.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2244\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=\frac{132\times 204}{\operatorname{HCF}(132,204)}=2244
\]

Fast result: \(2244\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

B. \(6998\)

D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[

\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

D. \(66\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=13\Rightarrow u=7
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 67.

\[
N=10\times 6+7=67
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 67 is 13.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(67\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=7
\]

\[
N=67
\]

Fast result: \(67\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q308. For exponent \(69\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(14\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(69-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 12.

\[
17^{69}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(69-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
4^{69}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q313. Between \(179\) and \(331\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 179 is 180.

\[
18\times 10=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 331.

\[
331=18\times 18+7
\]

\[
331-7=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{324-180}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{331}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{178}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-9=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q314. Two buses leave a stand every \(60\) and \(120\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(114\)
B. \(120\)
C. \(126\)
D. \(132\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
120=2^{3}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=120
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(120\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=\frac{60\times 120}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,120)}=120
\]

Fast result: \(120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Fast result: \(35\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q333. What is the least perfect cube that is divisible by \(108\)?
A. \(227\)
B. \(238\)
C. \(205\)
D. \(216\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
108=2^{2}\times 3^{3}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{3}\to 3^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
108\times 2=216
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=216
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(216\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(216\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\[

\[

Q362. The power \(3^{49}\) is very large. What are its final two digits?
A. \(82\)
B. \(85\)
C. \(83\)
D. \(84\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 27\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 43\rightarrow 29\rightarrow 87\rightarrow 61\rightarrow 83\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 47\rightarrow 41\rightarrow 23\rightarrow 69\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 21\rightarrow 63\rightarrow 89\rightarrow 67\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(49-1)\bmod 20+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 83.

\[
3^{49}\equiv 83\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 83.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(83\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(49-1)\bmod 20+1=9
\]

\[
3^{49}\equiv 83\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(83\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q366. Between \(230\) and \(356\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 230 is 234.

\[
9\times 26=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 351 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 356.

\[
356=9\times 39+5
\]

\[
356-5=351
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{351-234}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{356}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{229}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
39-25=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q372. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(11085466\)
B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(84,204)=1428
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1428.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1428\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1428\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q383. The number \(51x38\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q389. Between \(175\) and \(289\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 175 is 180.

\[
9\times 20=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 289.

\[
289=9\times 32+1
\]

\[
289-1=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{288-180}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{289}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{174}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
32-19=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q393. The number \(14x29\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
4+x=11
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4+7=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q395. Power \(7^{25}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 1.

\[
0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(25-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
7^{25}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(25-1)\bmod 1+1=1
\]

\[
0^{25}\equiv 0\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q413. The number \(158x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q416. For exponent \(75\), what ending three digits does \(8^{75}\) have?
A. \(454\)
B. \(475\)
C. \(410\)
D. \(432\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{1000}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 24.

\[
8\rightarrow 64\rightarrow 512\rightarrow 96\rightarrow 768\rightarrow 144\rightarrow 152\rightarrow 216\rightarrow 728\rightarrow 824\rightarrow 592\rightarrow 736\rightarrow 888\rightarrow 104\rightarrow 832\rightarrow 656\rightarrow 248\rightarrow 984\rightarrow 872\rightarrow 976\rightarrow 808\rightarrow 464\rightarrow 712\rightarrow 696
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(75-1)\bmod 24+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 512.

\[
8^{75}\equiv 512\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(432\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(75-1)\bmod 24+1=3
\]

\[
8^{75}\equiv 512\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(432\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q419. Between \(211\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(21\)
B. \(24\)
C. \(22\)
D. \(23\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 211 is 216.

\[
6\times 36=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=6\times 57+5
\]

\[
347-5=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{342-216}{6}+1=21+1=22
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 22.
Hence, there are 22 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(22\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{210}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
57-35=22
\]

Fast result: \(22\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q423. The number \(6x563\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=8
\]

\[
8-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
8-x=0
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
8-8=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q424. Two cyclic events repeat every \(126\) and \(306\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(2356\)
B. \(2570\)
C. \(1928\)
D. \(2142\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
126=2\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

\[
306=2\times 3^{2}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,306)=2142
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 2142.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2142\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(126,306)=\frac{126\times 306}{\operatorname{HCF}(126,306)}=2142
\]

Fast result: \(2142\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q425. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

\[

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q443. Reduce the power \(7^{59}\) by its remainder pattern. What is the remainder on division by \(19\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(59-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{59}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(59-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{59}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q447. Between \(125\) and \(212\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 125 is 128.

\[
8\times 16=128
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 208 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 212.

\[
212=8\times 26+4
\]

\[
212-4=208
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{208-128}{8}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{212}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{124}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-15=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q452. Number \(47\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(29\)
B. \(27\)
C. \(28\)
D. \(26\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+7=47
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 7+4=74
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-7|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-7|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q459. The number \(3x214\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 10; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
10+x=18
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
10+8=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

C. \(0\)

Answer: A

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

13^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(90-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{90}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q472. Between \(174\) and \(336\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 174 is 180.

\[
15\times 12=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 330 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 336.

\[
336=15\times 22+6
\]

\[
336-6=330
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{330-180}{15}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{336}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{173}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-11=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q478. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[

\]

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[

\]

Shortcut

\[

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q489. The number \(2x094\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 4, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
4\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

\[
N=60\times q=900
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 900.
\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(900\).

Q500. By what least number should \(360\) be multiplied to make it a perfect cube?
A. \(76\)
B. \(74\)
C. \(77\)
D. \(75\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
360=2^{3}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a cube, every exponent must be a multiple of 3.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{3}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{3}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{3}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=75
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=75
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(75\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=75
\]

Fast result: \(75\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_cube_factor_constraint
